# Novo Nordisk: Initiate at Underperform: Knife Still Falling?

Although the stock is close to five year lows, we don't think the EPS downgrade cycle is over.
Moreover, our bull-bear analysis is negatively skewed and the catalyst cupboard looks bare to
us. We also don't believe that the 10x PE26 implied by our Price Target is extreme as it implies
premia to stocks with deeper moats like Sanofi (O) on global sales regression analysis.

Why do we project up to 29% downside to the consensus '26-36 EPSe? We expect the
US obesity market to peak at $85bn in 2036, but our group sales forecasts are upto 18% <
consensus (BBG throughout) as we expect approved drugs (in particular Ozempic, Wegovy,
and Oral Sema) and the pipeline (mostly CagriSema) to underperform in the US due to lower
volume share & more price deflation. We also expect the US semaglutide compound patent
expiry in 2032 to drive greater mix deterioration and thus more margin contraction.

What's the risk reward? Our bull-bear analysis implies +43% upside vs -53% downside
to our '35e EBIT (DCF proxy). The approved portfolio accounts for 11% of the upside and
discounts Novo delivering consensus for Ozempic, Wegovy, and Oral Sema. Similarly, the
remaining 32% upside from pipeline discounts CagriSema consensus (but unlike 'the market'
we believe this is a niche, rather than mass consumer, obesity and diabetes drug).

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

Why can't we find any material catalysts? We don't expect new data to inflect
expectations for CagriSema. Moreover, considering that amycretin phase 3 data isn't
expected until 2028/2029 we believe this will also just be a niche product. And we can't get
excited by ziltivekimab (phase 3 data due in 2027) due to competitive crowding in IL-6.

## Investment Implications

Why's a 10x target PE appropriate? We calculate our Price Target via the average of our
DCF (DKK 191; 8% WACC & 0% perpetuity growth) and '26-'28 EV/EBITA multiples (DKK
160; 36% discount to EU peers) to be consistent with our existing EU biopharma coverage.
Our PT implies a 10x PE26 for discounts to EU peers, Lilly (O; Courtney Breen) and Novo's
20-year history of 40%, 67% and 61% respectively. (On PE30 the PT discounts to EU peers
and Lilly are 30% and 53% respectively). We believe these implied relative valuations are
robust considering Novo's below average growth, portfolio concentration risk and mixed
track record. Value investors should heed that our Novo PT implies a premium to Sanofi (O)
on our global '26e EV/sales to sales CAGR (26-31e) regression even though the latter enjoys
a deeper moat because of its large vaccines franchise.

## DETAILS

### FUNDAMENTALS DEEP DIVE: NOVO NORDISK (UP; UNDERPERFORM); WHY'S THE KNIFE STILL FALLING?

#### WHY DO WE PROJECT BELOW AVERAGE GROWTH?

We project Novo Nordisk to deliver sales growth below EU biopharma peers 2026-2031 inclusive (Exhibit 1) for two reasons:

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

1/Semaglutide loss of exclusivity: In 2025, products containing semaglutide (Ozempic, Rybelsus, and Wegovy) accounted
for 74% of Novo's group sales. Novo has hitched its growth prospects on the Wegovy Pill (oral semaglutide) and CagriSema
(cagrilintide + semaglutide) over the medium term. Incorporating loss of exclusivity (LOE) in key international markets in 2026
(Canada, Brazil, India, and China), and compound patent expiries in the EU and US in 2031 and 2032 (respectively), we estimate
that 1/3 of group sales are at risk of generic competition in 2026, rising to 2/3 in 2032 (Exhibit 3).

2/Weakening positions in obesity and diabetes markets: Having entered the GLP-1 market for both diabetes (2017,
through Ozempic) and obesity (2021, through Wegovy), Novo's semaglutide has lost considerable market share to Lilly in both
diabetes (vs Mounjaro) and obesity (vs Zepbound). We think these dynamics are entrenched and see no prospect of a share
reversal. We forecast - 12% group sales growth in 2026 (driven by US price deflation) and then anemic growth across to 2033
(Exhibit 1).

We project EPS growth for Novo below EU peers '26-29e inclusive (Exhibit 2). The variance is wider than for sales because
we forecast margin contraction caused by US deflation (creating a negative mix effect in the diabetes and obesity GLP-1
franchises) and semaglutide patent expiries across key regions ex. US.

Consequently, we forecast an EPS CAGR of 5% '26-2031e and 0.6% '32-36e. In our view, US pricing headwinds in obesity
and diabetes GLP-1 markets will lead to a -24% drop in EPS in 2026 (Exhibit 5). Over the mid-term (until 2031) we believe that
products such as the Wegovy Pill (Oral Sema), CagriSema (which is due to launch in both obesity and diabetes), and increased
penetration of the US obesity markets can help deliver mid-single digit (MSD) EPS growth pre the LOE (loss of exclusivity) of
semaglutide in the US (which is due in 2032).

Post the semaglutide US compound patent expiry in 2032 we expect EPS to almost flat-line until 2036 (which is the
end of our bottom up forecast period). This is because we do not think Novo's remaining approved portfolio and pipeline will be
able to mitigate the semaglutide patent expiries.

##### We do not think the Wegovy Pill and CagriSema launches will be enough to offset the loss of sales from Ozempic and Wegovy. We thus do not expect Novo to deliver sustainable group sales growth between 2025 and 2031e (Exhibit 6).

Between 2026 and 2031 we project cumulative sales for the Wegovy Pill and CagriSema (Novo's highest profile launch prior
to the 2032 US patent expiry) of DKK60bn (Exhibit 6). But over this period we expect DKK 90bn of cumulative lost sales from
Ozempic and Wegovy. From 2026-31E, we thus forecast that the Wegovy Pill will account for 40% of group sales CAGR (Exhibit
7).

# Novo's Earnings Story so Far: Meteoric Rise, Unfortunate Fall

Why did Novo deliver a sustained period of earnings upgrades? To recap, Novo was an early player in the GLP-1 market,
with Victoza (active ingredient liraglutide) gaining FDA approval in 2010. This was the first once-daily human GLP-1 approved
for type 2 diabetes.

1\. GLP1 penetration of US diabetes market. Novo launched Ozempic in the US in 2017, a once weekly GLP-1 for type 2
diabetes management. Since then, GLP-1s penetration in the US diabetes market has steadily increased (Exhibit 9). In our
view this is primarily because, unlike insulin, these products lead to weight loss rather than weight gain.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

2\. Inflection of the US obesity market for GLP-1s. The weight-loss benefits enjoyed by patients on Ozempic increased
investors' expectations of the potential for GLP-1s to be used as broader weight management medications - with many
patients using Ozempic "off-label" (i.e. for weight-loss and not for diabetes management). Subsequently, data from the
phase 3 SELECT outcomes trial in 3Q23 inflected perceptions of the potential of GLP-1s in weight loss. This is because in
SELECT Wegovy lowered cardiovascular risk (a composite of reducing heart attacks, strokes, and other CV related deaths)
by 20%. This proved that GLP-1s deliver medicinal value beyond weight-loss.

What has caused this downgrade cycle? There have been three major events in our view:

. Poor execution on manufacturing. We think Novo's strategy for semaglutide production is a prime study for "failure to
prepare is preparation to fail". Here is a recap of key events:

. Novo let demand for semaglutide products (namely Ozempic and Wegovy) run ahead of their (known) installed capacity
for quality checking and fill-finishing. In March 2022, the FDA announced a shortage of semaglutide, under section 503b.
This led to compounding pharmacies entering the US market to ease the semaglutide shortage.

· Novo has invested $25bn since 2022 in capex to bolster fill-finish (putting the active pharmaceutical ingredient (API)
into pen injection devices). Then, in early 2025, the FDA announced that the semaglutide shortage was over. However,
compounding pharmacies have proven very difficult to remove from the market. Our channel checks indicate are these
drugs still command 1/3 of the US market on a volume basis.

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="5" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

. We think Novo has suffered heavily on three fronts: A/loss of Ozempic and Wegovy sales 2022-present, B/greater than
expected capex investments from a position of relative weakness, and C/ allowing competitors (compounders and Lilly) to
take share.

. Three profit warnings in 2025 and another one in 2026 (Exhibit 10). Novo cut its sales and EBIT growth guidance for
FY25 three times across 2025 (at 1Q, 2Q, and 3Q), citing lower penetration of GLP-1s in the US (1Q), in international markets
(2Q), and the slower than expected exit of compounders (1Q, 2Q and 3Q). Management also announced a large restructuring
program in early September 2025, cutting 9k jobs. Then the first wave of public channel GLP-1 price cuts in the US led
by The White House in November contributed to the guidance cut at 3Q25. Novo is now guiding for negative group sales
growth at constant currency (in the 5-13% range) in 2026 because of more US price cuts needed to stimulate long term
volume growth.

. CagriSema disappointment(s) eroding confidence in the pipeline. CagriSema was initially billed as Novo's answer
to tirzepatide (active ingredient in Lilly's Mounjaro (diabetes) and Zepbound (obesity)). More specifically, Novo had hoped
CagriSema (cagrilintide (amylin) + semaglutide) could achieve 25% weight loss. (For context, Zepbound delivered 21%,
placebo adjusted weight loss, in the phase 3 SURMOUNT-1 obesity trials). But in REDEFINE-1 (which read out December
2024), CagriSema disappointed investors, achieving 20.4% placebo adjusted weight-loss. In REDEFINE-4, which was
a head-to-head trial of CagriSema vs Zepbound (which read out in February 2026) the former failed to meet its primary
endpoint of non-inferiority (i.e. it was inferior to Zepbound for weight loss). We believe this chain of events has dented
investors' confidence in Novo's R&D execution.

We forecast upto 29% downside to the consensus EPS forecasts as we expect top line underperformance to be
exacerbated by worse profitability and lower cash generation (Exhibit 11).

On the top line we forecast material downside vs consensus across Novo's five biggest franchises (three of which
accounted for 74% of 2025 group sales). For the products currently on the market (Ozempic, Wegovy, and Oral Sema), we sit
30%, 15%, and 13% below consensus respectively (Exhibit 12).

· Our Wegovy estimates (read more here) assume a +4% CAGR (26-36e) of US prescriptions' ('script'; volume) and - 10%
annual US price declines across all channels across 2027-36e. We think the current negative share trends vs Zepbound

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="6" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->
<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->

will continue and that Wegovy US price declines will not cause mid-to-long term volumes to surprise on the upside. All in all,
we think consensus is too bullish on both Wegovy US TRx (prescription) volume growth and the magnitude of on US price
declines.

. Our Oral Sema ('Wegovy in a Pill') forecast for 2026 (launch year) is 25% below consensus (more here). From 2027
onwards, we project further downside to consensus. Similarly to our Wegovy forecasts, we are more conservative on US
prescription volumes than consensus (we think Oral Sema will command 10% of TRx volumes in the US obesity market in
2032 and 7% in 2036; Exhibit 84).

. Our Ozempic projections (see more here) assume -2% US script growth until 2032 (when loss of exclusivity occurs) and
then -3%-5% volume declines until 2036. In 2026 and 2027 we assume US deflation of 30% and 15% respectively. We also
model - 10% US price declines from 2028-2036.

. We also expect CagriSema (obesity and diabetes) to underperform consensus. This is because we view this as a niche, not
a mass market, drug in both diabetes and obesity. We forecast a 3% share of US TRx volumes in 2036e in the US obesity
and diabetes markets respectively. Despite the recent disappointing REDEFINE-4 headline data, in our view, consensus still
hasn't properly discounted CagriSema's likely niche market position in our view.

## We project double-digit downgrades to the consensus EPSe until 2033 (Exhibit 13)due to:

1\. Lower group sales growth projections: we forecast weaker US volume growth and more US deflation for GLP-1s in both
obesity and diabetes, with a more bearish outlook on Novo's pipeline assets (as discussed above).

2\. More aggressive margin contraction: we believe that consensus is underestimating the level of margin contraction which
will occur post the semaglutide US compound patent expiry in 2032. In our view, Novo will likely suffer from a combination
of a) worsening product mix and b) SG&A headwinds (Exhibit 14).

. We forecast a greater mix hit at COGS because we think the injectables vs orals share split will impair product mix. As
semaglutide is a peptide (short chain protein), it suffers from low bio-availability in the oral formulation. Whilst we think this
will help protect volumes against generic erosion, we believe this will come at a cost at the margin level (Oral Sema is more
expensive to make as it contains much more active pharmaceutical ingredient (API) than Wegovy does).

. We also forecast a profitability hit at SG&A because we believe that this line contains significant fixed costs. Hence, we do
not believe that Novo will be able to deliver a percentage reduction in absolute SG&A costs which will mirror the percentage
decline at group sales).

### WHAT'S THE RISK REWARD?

#### An Overview of Our Bull-Bear Scenario Analysis

We have constructed a bull-bear scenario approach for Novo sales and EBIT impact across to 2035 (we believe 2035e EBIT is
a good DCF proxy). We have focused our bull-bear on the key portfolio products (Ozempic, Wegovy, Oral Sema) and major late
stage pipeline assets (CagriSema in obesity and diabetes). In Exhibit 27 we provide a summary table.

#### Our bull scenario:

##### · +29% upside to 2035E sales and 43% upside to 2035E EBIT

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

. Considering the variance of Bernstein vs consensus across portfolio and pipeline assets, we have taken consensus
estimates as our "bull case" for Ozempic, Wegovy, Oral Sema, and CagriSema. In Exhibit 15 and Exhibit 16 we illustrate
how our sales estimates compare to consensus for 2035e sales and EBIT respectively.

. In the approved portfolio, we project upsides of 4%, +2%, and +2% to Ozempic, Wegovy, and Oral Sema 2035E sales
respectively. The corresponding EBIT upsides are +5%, +3%, and +3% respectively.

. In the pipeline, our bull case implies +12% and +10% upside to 2035E sales for CagriSema in obesity and diabetes
respectively. The corresponding EBIT upsides are +17% and +14% respectively. But we believe that consensus is still
too optimistic on the positioning of CagriSema in both obesity and diabetes (we believe consensus is unwisely discounting
mass-market, rather than niche, use). We forecast peak US CagriSema TRx volumes to reach 3% of the market in diabetes
and obesity respectively (Exhibit 84 and Exhibit 92).

#### Our bear scenario:

##### . - 36% downside to 2035E sales and -53% downside to EBIT

· To model downside scenarios, we have softened the assumptions in our underlying US obesity and diabetes models. In
Exhibit 15 and Exhibit 16 we illustrate how our core forecasts compare to our bear case estimates for sales and EBIT
respectively.

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="8" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

. For the on-market products, we model US TRx and price dynamics and make a revenue adjustment for ex. US sales. We
assume faster US volume declines for Ozempic and Wegovy (-2% and -5% respectively) across 2030-35E and US price
deflation towards the top end of the normal range in the diabetes and obesity markets (-15% and -20% yoy respectively
across from 2026). For Oral Sema, we have modeled slower penetration (5% US TRx growth over 2030-35E) and the
same price deflation as Wegovy (-20% yoy). All in all, this results in respective downsides to 2035E sales of -4%, -9%,
and -9% (Exhibit 27). The corresponding respective downside at 2035e EBIT are -6%, -13%, and - 14%(also Exhibit 27).

· In the pipeline, we modeled a worst-case scenario of phase 3 projects not launching (i.e. O sales across 2026-36E.
Collectively, this implied - 14% sales risk and -20% EBIT risk (both in 2035E).

Where do we see the Most Risk to our Bull-Bear analysis?
The component of our bearish investment thesis which alerts us most is the potential for Oral Sema out-performance
vs consensus. We have dedicated significant bandwidth to derive our assumptions for US volume growth, US price deflation
and US market share (all of which can be read in depth here). Hence, we believe that they are robust.

In the short term ('26-27E) our bull, core, and bear scenarios are somewhat similar to consensus in terms of sales and
TRx share of the US obesity market. We provide more detail on Oral Sema vs orforglipron here. In our view, the launch of Oral
Sema in the US (January 2026) will not be able to mitigate aggressive US price cuts across Novo's broader obesity portfolio and
drive top-line growth into 2026 and 2027. We project that group revenues will not grow next year.

Over the medium term, we think Oral Sema will command a peak 11% share of US obesity prescriptions (with the
peak coming in 2030e). We think consensus is much more bullish on where Oral Sema will land over the medium term (Exhibit
20). For clarity, in our 2031e base case, we estimate the US obesity market shares by volumes for 'high profile' tablets, 'high
profile' injectable GLP1s and 'others' (ie players other than Novo and Lilly, but including compounders) to be 34%, 44% and
23% respectively. Within the 34% we expect to orforglipron (Lilly (O) to outperform Oral Sema by 2 to 1.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

We are mindful of Novo's ambitions for Wegovy 7.2mg (high dose Wegovy). In November 2025, Novo submitted Wegovy
(7.2mg) to the FDA for approval, using the Commissioner's National Priority Voucher (CNPV) gained as part of The White
House deal (announced in November 2025). The CNPV reduces the FDA review time from 10-12 months to 1-2 months, from
acceptance of the submission. Novo thus expects an FDA decision in 2Q26.

But we don't believe new trial data is enough to shift share dynamics. Data from the STEP-UP trial (published September
2025) demonstrated that the higher Wegovy dose (semaglutide 7.2mg) did increase patient weight loss vs semaglutide 2.4mg
(Exhibit 21). We note, however, that headline weight loss was still below that of Zepbound. Thus, we do not believe the higher
Wegovy dose has the potential to inflect US volume (script) trends materially. In our view, Zepbound remains the most effective
weight loss drug on the US market, with a better tolerance profile. We expect both patients and physicians to share this view.
Hence, we believe that script trends will continue to reflect patients' preference for Zepbound over Wegovy.

Our "bull-case" discounts consensus and implies +4% upside to our 2035e EBIT. We assume that the consensus
estimates include Wegovy 7.2mg sales. Including the consensus' US Wegovy sales into our US obesity model, we estimate that
consensus assumes Wegovy will have a benign share loss vs 2025 prescription volumes (Exhibit 22 (refer to the numbers inside

To conclude, it is the accumulation of several smaller differences that lands us well below consensus at group sales.
We are also very comfortable with our views on the value propositions for each of these products. 1/ we believe that Ozempic
will continue to US lose share vs Mounjaro (Lilly), and 2/we also believe that CagriSema will be a niche obesity and diabetes
medication (we assume a peak of 3% US volume share in each indication).

# WHAT ARE THE PIPELINE MILESTONES?

We expect benign news flow with very few drug catalysts during the next few years (Exhibit 28).

. CagriSema: too little, too late. The REDEFINE-4 (CagriSema vs Zepbound head-to-head) headline read out has already
happened (February 2026; click here to be taken to Novo's press room). This means that CagriSema has now delivered
two sets of disappointing phase 3 results (REDEFINE-1 and REDEFINE-4). Hence, we have not included REDEFINE-11
(longer duration trial with more aggressive dose escalation, read out expected in 1H27) in our catalyst calendar. Moreover,
we now don't believe that CagriSema can achieve better (or even equivalent) weight loss to Zepbound - regardless of what is
reported in REDEFINE-11.

. Amycretin: too early to provide material upside. Novo's other obesity pipeline prospect has only just entered phase 3
trials. Amycretin has a dual mechanism of action (GLP-1 + amylin analogue) and we acknowledge the phase 2 data thus far
has been solid (Exhibit 44). But we also recognize that Amycretin will be entering a much more crowded obesity market. We
estimate a 2029 launch, by which time there could be 9 major obesity medications on the market (see our obesity market
summary page here).

· Ziltivekimab: not differentiated enough. This drug is a fully human monoclonal antibody which targets interleukin 6 (IL-6)
to manage cardiovascular disease, chronic kidney disease, and systemic inflammation. (IL-6 helps cells to communicate
with each other of which one (of many outputs) is regulation of glucose (simple sugar) and lipid (fat) metabolism). But we
understand that there are five other IL-6 inhibitors in phase 3. In addition, Actemra (Roche; O) has the same mechanism of
action but is now facing biosimilar competition so potentially could get used 'off label' to manage cardiometabolic conditions.
(Considering that this drug was launched in the US in 2010 it benefits from many years of safe patient use).

## Why else aren't we excited about the late stage pipeline?

In the short term Novo Nordisk intends to launch Mim8 for use hemophilia A. These patients are at risk of (potentially fatal)
uncontrolled bleeding events because they cannot produce enough Factor VIII (protein which facilitates blood clotting) on their
own. Mim8 has the same mechanism of action as Roche's Hemlibra (launched in 2017; 2025 sales $5bn).

Once administered to hemophiliacs, Hemlibra behaves like naturally occurring Factor VIII (the drug is a mimetic). Mim8 has the
same bispecific antibody mechanism of action (in effect the drug's 'two keys' fit into two different padlocks inside the body) and
was invented by Genmab (UP). Novo in-licensed this drug in 2015.

We acknowledge that from the 'get go' Mim8 will be available in a prefilled syringe (PFS) which can be directly injected under
the skin (subcutaneous formulation). Hemlibra is also administered subcutaneously but via vial and syringe so is arguably less
convenient. However, we believe that Hemlibra's 9 years of safe use represents a formidable barrier to entry to Mim8. We also

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="14" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

that note Roche and Chugai (O; Miki Sogi) are about to start a phase 3 trial of Hemlibra vs their own next generation Factor VIII
mimetic called NXT007. In phase 1 this drug was 30x more effective than Hemlibra. But in phase 3 Novo Nordisk didn't directly
compare Mim8 to Hemlibra.

All things considered, we do not believe that Mim8 can materially alter Novo's mid to long term sales growth trajectory. Our
2035 sales estimate for this drug of DKK 4.4 bn is 60% below consensus (Bloomberg). We believe that other commentators are
either underestimating Hemlibra's first mover advantage and/or the potential competitive threat from NXT007.

Between 2026 and 2031 we forecast that Novo Nordisk will generate $60bn in post-tax free cash flow. We also project 2026
and 2027 EBITDA of $17bn and $19bn respectively and that the company will be 'net cash' in 2031 (Exhibit 29). Moreover, at
31/12/25 the company's net debt/EBITDA was 0.7x which is far below peak financial leverage in EU Pharma (at the bookend
Shire geared its balance sheet to 6x in 2016 to facilitate the acquisition of Baxalta). Hence, in our view, theoretically Novo
Nordisk has the financial resources to engage in material 'bridging' M&A. (Recent radical examples include AstraZeneca's (O)
$3bn rights issue in 2Q19 to fund a 50/50 partnership with Daiichi Sankyo (O; Miki Sogi)) on Enhertu (HER2 breast cancer) and
Genmab's (UP) $9bn acquisition of cancer specialist Merus last year. Both of these transactions involved late stage assets which
were/are close to market).

We acknowledge that in 2025 Novo Nordisk spent $11bn to acquire three former Catalent manufacturing plants from the Novo
Foundation. But in our view this was a 'one off' transaction to alleviate Novo Nordisk's GLP1 manufacturing constraints. But
history suggests that, for pipeline bolstering purposes, Novo tends to adopt a conservative approach to M&A which strongly
favors drugs in early to mid-stage clinical development. We hope that we're wrong, but considering this track record and the
composition of Novo's Board of Directors (Exhibit 30), we'd be surprised if this the company's strategic approach changes
radically going forwards.

# WHAT'S THE STOCK WORTH?

Consistent with our approach to valuing the other stocks in Bernstein's EU pharma coverage universe, we calculate our price
target (PT) via a straight average of 2026-2028e EV/EBITA multiples and a DCF. In the latter, our bottom-up forecasts extend to
2036 after which we apply fade rates until 2045. We thus only apply a perpetuity growth rate (of 0% for an exit multiple of
12.5x) in 2045. This means that the exit value accounts for just 23% of the total EV.

## Could Novo become a single-digit PE stock? Not Quite in our view

In our EV/EBITA valuation, we assume that the stock should trade at a 36% discount to EU peers to account for the portfolio
concentration risk, meek M&A heritage, sustained below average sales growth and poor operational execution.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

Compared to EU peers: Our PT implies an adjusted ('core') PE26 of 10x for a -40% discount. Meanwhile, in 2030 the implied
discount is -30% (Exhibit 33). Our PT also implies that Novo trades on a 15-year low PE multiple (Exhibit 34). We believe this
is appropriate considering the significant challenge which the company faces in returning to sustainable above sector growth
following the semaglutide US compound patent expiry in 2032 and the company's weakened competitive positioning in the
obesity and diabetes markets.

Relative to Lilly: our Novo PT implies a -67% discount to Lilly in 2026 and a -53% discount in 2030 (Exhibit 35). This also
implies a 15-year low PE relative to Lilly (Exhibit 36). We believe this is warranted considering Lilly's far better operational and
R&D execution in metabolic diseases and its superior therapeutic diversification. (Lilly launched its first GLP1 in diabetes in the
US four years after Novo did).

At our Price Target, Novo would still trade at a premium to Sanofi (O) on PE26 even though the latter's business model is more
durable as it includes a large vaccines franchise (which, in our view, enjoys the deepest moat in all of biopharma due to the
highest capex, toughest R&D, and no generic risk; Exhibit 37). We thus do not think that our Novo PT is extreme.

# US OBESITY MARKET: SOLID VOLUME GROWTH OFFSETTING STICKY DEFLATION

The obesity market is already worth $25bn in 2025. We forecast a 12% value CAGR over the next decade, where the US
obesity market will be worth $85bn (Exhibit 42). So far, this story has been dominated by the share battle between Novo and
Lilly (O; covered by Courtney Breen). But even with new entrants arriving at the end of the decade, we believe the market will
remain a duopoly, with Lilly the dominant player (Exhibit 43).

Our key takeaway from the past five years is simple: in injectables, weight loss wins. Tolerance profile and whether the
drug comes with restrictions (e.g. fasting before taking it) are of secondary importance - headline weight loss is the primary
needle mover. We think this is largely for two reasons.

1\. Obesity drugs are prescribed in primary care settings, where practitioners are relatively time poor. Practitioners will
prescribe their "favored" GLP-1 and only move patients onto a different product should their side effects become
intolerable.

2\. The direct-to-consumer channel in the US has increased the importance of headline weight loss. Patients seeking to lose
weight (reasonably) want the product which maximizes the speed and magnitude of their weight loss. The tolerance profile
is a "second order" consideration, whereby a degree of discomfort is tolerated for additional weight loss. (Proven 'outcomes'
benefits such as stroke reduction are even further down the list).

We think the market dynamics are tough to disrupt. We don't foresee a share reversal back to 2023 or 2024 levels (where
Novo had 60% of the US obesity market). Nor do we think a major disruptor is waiting in the wings. Instead, we think Lilly will
remain the dominant player, Novo the (distant) number two, and a tail of players with smaller shares. In our view, both Lilly and
Novo should benefit from existing positions on the market, which should be protected by pipeline launches.

In our obesity model, we have used Exhibit 44 to guide our thinking about the quality of obesity drugs. We have tried to
normalize weight loss and discontinuation rates (due to adverse events) between studies (which run for varying levels of time)
by taking averages of both measures across trial length. We recognize this is an imperfect tool, and readers should be wary
of indirect comparisons between drugs due a variety of trial design differences. But we still believe this provides an intuitive
understanding of how weight loss drugs (on the market and in the pipeline) compare to each other. We also note that numerous
trials are run on each drug (with differing results for weight-loss and tolerance). We have provided trial names (where available)

## PRICING: FY26'S DOMINANT THEME

The US price environment has deteriorated faster than expected. Between compounders undercutting, growth in the
direct-to-consumer cash channel, and agreements with the US government, we believe that prices will fall far quicker than is
usual in pharmaceuticals this year. We expect returns to "normal" levels of deflation (10-20% each year) to return from 2027
onwards.

### Recap: TrumpRx and The White House Deal

In November 2025, Novo and Lilly agreed to the White House's drug pricing deal terms for GLP-1s. Here, we outline
briefly what the deal contained and how it has informed our modeling assumptions. For a more in-depth look at what the deal
means, you can read our US colleague Courtney Breen's note from just after the deal was announced here.

Novo and Lilly benefited from:

1\. Medicare and Medicaid coverage for obesity drugs (for both on-market and pipeline assets): Medicare (>65s) and
Medicaid (low income) coverage comes with a $50 co-pay for patients. Expansion through the Medicare channel could add
18m new patients (with coverage limitations depending on patient BMI).

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="23" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->
<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->

2\. 3-year tariff exemption: Novo and Lilly will not be subject to tariff costs for three years.

3\. One Comissioner's National Priority Voucher (CNPV) each: This offers each company the opportunity to reduce FDA
review time from 10-12 months down to 1-2 months.

### In exchange for:

#### 1. Deep discounts for primary care (obesity and diabetes) drugs:

· Through TrumpRx (a direct-to-consumer channel for prescription drugs which launched in January 2026), Ozempic
and Wegovy are $350 (highest doses) and the Wegovy Pill is $150 for the initial dose ($299 for the highest dose). We
understand the deal also stipulated that injectables will trend towards a blended net price of $245 by the end of 2027.

· Through Medicare/Medicaid Ozempic, Wegovy, Mounjaro, and Zepbound will be $245 with a $50 co-pay - available
from April 2026. For Medicaid, this requires an opt-in from states to participate (California has not opted in).

2\. MFN pricing guarantees on all future new medicines: Lilly and Novo guaranteed the US market will receive MFN pricing
on future medicine, increasing pricing on existing products in ex. US markets, and using MFN pricing for all state Medicaid
programs.

3\. Large manufacturing investments into the US: Novo announced $10bn investment in manufacturing (primarily through
Wegovy Pill production). Lilly had previously announced $30bn of investment earlier in 2025.

##### How have we modeled pricing?

We have modeled pricing dynamics channel by channel for Wegovy, Zepbound, Oral Sema, and orforglipron in order to generate
a blended net price for each.

· Wegovy and Zepbound: We have applied a -30% price discount across Medicaid, Medicare, and Commercial (i.e. private
insurance) to our 4Q25 prices in to get our 1Q26 prices. We have then assumed a price of $245 in the Medicare and
Medicaid channels to take effects from 2Q26 until 4Q26. After applying a - 10% discount on our 4Q25 estimates of
commercial channel prices, we keep commercial channel prices flat across the rest of 2026. In the cash pay channel,
we have calculated the average price (weighted by dosage volumes) using TrumpRx and the cash channel sites of Novo
(NovoCare) and Lilly (LillyDirect), we have assumed no "in-year" price changes here. Going forward, we assume - 10% yoy
price erosion across each channel. Due to the channel mix (i.e. more prescriptions going through higher paying channels
(Commercial), blended prices don't immediately normalize to - 10% (prices normalize around - 10% in 2030).

· Oral Sema: We have created a dose-weighted (using the prescription data available) average price and assumed the only
active channel in 1Q26 is the cash channel - the price seems artificially low because most scripts are coming through for
the lowest (i.e. cheapest) dose. We assume these same dynamics as other channels come into play across the year - with the

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="24" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->
<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->

commercial channel having a slight premium. We assume prices normalize towards a blended net price of $245 by 4Q26 and
then apply - 10% yoy.

. Orforglipron: We have taken the price assumptions from our US colleague Courtney Breen for 2026. We have then applied
the same - 10% price erosion assumptions yoy thereon.

### How have we modeled prescription volumes?

We forecast +50% TRx (total prescription; 'scripts') volume growth for the US obesity market in '26 (Exhibit 42).

Despite the price cuts announced for this following year, we forecast a modest TRx volume uptick. Following +111% growth in
total TRx volumes in FY25 (both branded (Wegovy and Zepbound) and compounding pharmacy TRx volumes grew at +111%),
we forecast +63% growth in branded injectables (Wegovy and Zepbound), -25% growth in compounding TRx volumes, and
strong oral launches to drive overall US obesity market TRx volumes to +50% yoy in 2026.

We expect the real "uptick" in total TRx volumes to materialize in FY27 (+40% total TRx volume growth in the US
obesity market). This represents a -9% slowing in the market growth rate. Relative to previous years in obesity, and other
pharma markets, this represents a material increase in script volumes. We think increased penetration of the US cash channel
and increased access to Medicare and Medicaid patients to materialize in 2027 scripts and are key for driving penetration of the
US obesity market.

We forecast +25% growth in Wegovy scripts in FY26 but, given our pricing assumptions, this translates into -27%
yoy US sales growth (Exhibit 47):

. Price cuts impact the top-line immediately, while TRx uplifts only accrue in the future. +25% yoy script growth still
represents a more begin slowdown in TRx growth than we have seen historically. In our view, Lilly's market position (40%
of TRx volumes and growing) means it can shoulder -30% price cuts and maintain positive sales growth through FY26. We
don't think Novo will be able to take enough share of a) "organic" new patient starts, b) compounding patients moving to
branded medications, or c) patients using Lilly's Zepbound to deliver positive Wegovy US sales growth in FY26.

. The early evidence shows that price cuts can increase volumes ... for Lilly. We are careful to mix causation and
correlation (there is a strong underlying structural trend supporting Zepbound (Exhibit 48) that shouldn't be understated)

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="25" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

but all evidence of price cuts so far suggest to us that the benefits fall disproportionately to Lilly. Consumers (and physicians)
seem to view the slightly higher price for Zepbound (c$669 vs $625 blended net prices in FY25) as worth it, given the
increased weight loss vs Wegovy (-21% vs - 15% weight loss; SURMOUNT-1 & STEP-1 respectively Exhibit 55). We think this
perception of value is entrenched, so long as Lilly continues to keep prices within bound for the additional weight loss to be
deemed "worth it".

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

. Medicare and Medicaid coverage is a tailwind beginning in 2Q26, but we believe their importance may have been
overstated. We have modeled +50% and +30% TRx volume growth in the Medicare and Medicaid channels respectively
for Wegovy this year. In 4Q25, we estimate that Novo accounted for 41% of total Medicare and Medicare (combined)
prescription volumes. This had fallen from 64% in 1Q25 (-23% share loss). We forecast this trend to continue over the next
two years with - 12% and -6% share in FY26 and FY27 respectively.

. The cash-channel is much more sensitive to consumer sentiment and its price deflation spills into other channels.
In 2025, 15% of Wegovy prescriptions were derived from the cash channel (Exhibit 50). In early 2026, 30% of TRx came
via the cash channel. Going forward, we model the cash channel to continue to increase up to 40% by 2027, primarily at the
expense of the commercial (private insurance) channel (Exhibit 50).

Beyond FY26, we still do not think US Wegovy TRx growth will be enough to generate positive US revenue growth for
Novo. Price declines of 10-20% yoy are standard in the obesity market (take the price decline into FY25 in Exhibit 51) and we
have modeled - 10% yoy 2027-36. To deliver positive US revenue growth, TRx growth needs to run ahead of the rate of price
declines. But in our model, we do not forecast Wegovy TRx growth to return to >10% across the next decade (Exhibit 51). The
Wegovy molecule, semaglutide, loses compound patent protection (its most robust intellectual property) in 2032. This is why
we forecast volumes to negatively inflect. We also believe that we have been generous not to assume more aggressive price and
volume declines for this drug during this period.

Net net, we think price declines will erode 79% of the US market's (and 87% of Novo's) growth value over the next
decade. Exhibit 53 and Exhibit 54 show that, using today's net prices, our total prescription volume forecasts (across all on-
market and pipeline obesity products) would add $335bn in market value ($60bn to Novo) by 2036. But we expect Novo to
suffer particularly acutely from deflationary pressures due to the semaglutide loss of exclusivity (LOE) in the US in 2032, which
will allow generic players to enter the market (The US Zepbound compound patent doesn't expire until 2036 - Exhibit 52).

# THE STATE OF PLAY FOR 2026: THREE BIG THEMES BEYOND DEFLATION

1/ Wegovy vs Zepbound: Still Important, More of the Same

. In our view Wegovy will continue to lose share to Zepbound in the US. We think injectables (including compounders)
will account for 88% of prescriptions volumes in FY26 and injectables will remain the driving force behind the US obesity
market. We think Wegovy and Zepbound are competitively priced and patient perceptions about better weight loss (for
Zepbound) are now fully baked in (Exhibit 55). Therefore, we think a continued (but steady) share loss vs Zepbound is

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="27" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->
<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->

the most likely scenario. Across 2025, weekly Wegovy scripts were (almost) flat, while Zepbound has continued to build
momentum Exhibit 56.

· Wegovy's higher (7.2mg) dose will not be enough to reverse share losses. After submission using Novo's CNPV in
November 2025, management is expecting an FDA decision in 2Q26. We acknowledge the higher dose achieves weight loss
using semaglutide which is closer to tirzepatide (active ingredient in Lilly's Zepbound). But in our view Zepbound remains
more efficacious (and has a better tolerance profile (Exhibit 55)). Thus, we are unconvinced that a higher Wegovy dose will be
able to drive enough share capture of new or existing patients to materially inflect current share trends between Zepbound
and Wegovy (Exhibit 56). In our view, Lilly is managing Zepbound's life-cycle very effectively. This includes recently launching
the KwikPen, which contains four doses in the same pen. We also do not believe that the Wegovy 7.2mg has strong enough
weigh loss data to materially change patient and physician perceptions.

## We believe consensus is too bullish on TRx volumes or is not pricing in enough deflation

Using consensus revenue estimates for US Wegovy, we have backed out implied TRx and price assumptions. We thus believe
that consensus is either:

a) overestimating the potential for Wegovy Trx's to inflect in the short term

or
b) underestimating the amount of deflation coming into the market across the next decade.

In our view, consensus appears to be optimistic on both counts.

US prescriptions: We believe that consensus has overly bullish assumptions on FY26 US Wegovy script growth and
share loss to Zepbound. We have spoken already about our reasoning for expecting Wegovy to continue to lose share vs
Zepbound and why we think the expected TRx uplift will come in FY27, not FY26 (for example, lawsuits with compounders will
take longer to resolve in our view and increase in medicare/medicaid scripts will take time to inflect).

In Exhibit 57 we use the blended (by channel) net price assumption in our full Novo earnings model ($365 in FY26 and - 10%
annual declines 2027-36) to calculate the implied prescription volume trend. The prescription growth rates between Bernstein
and consensus are very similar until the LOE (where we model a benign erosion from generics; more here).

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="28" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

The main divergence between Bernstein and consensus is Wegovy US total prescriptions in FY26. We think consensus figures
imply 38% TRx growth in FY26. This would be higher yoy than FY25 (+36%). Given our discussion on TRx growth in FY26 and
FY27, we believe it is unlikely that growth in FY26 will be higher than FY25 (we opt for a softer decline in growth rates). Further,
plugging the implied consensus TRx figures into our obesity model, we think this implies minimal loss vs Zepbound in 2026. In
our view this is too bullish as we don't expect any near term milestones to inflect the 2025 trends.

Price: whilst we have modeled benign price deflation, consensus seems even more optimistic. In our 'base case' Novo
earnings model, we assume use a -10% yoy price decline for Wegovy in the US through 2027-36. We believe that leaves prices
low enough at the point of the LOE to mitigate aggressive generic entry. But we believe that consensus may be underestimating
the level of deflation coming into the market across the next decade.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

In Exhibit 58 we use our own US Wegovy TRx assumptions (as shown in Exhibit 51) to back out the consensus implied blended
net price. Our assumptions imply a 42% yoy blended net price decline (-30% on our 4Q25 prices). We think consensus is pricing
in a -35% price decline in 2026. Given that the majority of prices have been announced (i.e. Medicare/Medicaid/TRumpRx), we
believe consensus is underestimating spillovers into the commercial (typically the highest-priced) channel.

Net net, this implies a much lower net price in FY26. But we also think consensus is discounting softer declines through to the
LOE (HSD declines). Hence, in our view, consensus is being too optimistic on the impact of deteriorating mix. We expect the
lowest price channel (i.e. 'cash') to consistently grow much quicker than the highest price (i.e. 'commercial') channel.

# 2/ Orals Entering the US Obesity Market

. The Wegovy Pill (oral semaglutide) launched in the US in January 2026. So far, the launch has exceeded expectations
(weekly TRx sit well above Zepbound launch; Exhibit 59). Early indications also indicate that oral semaglutide is expanding
the obesity market (given such a large % are on starter doses; Exhibit 60) rather than cannibalizing the injectables market.

. We have used our own net price assumptions channel by channel for FY26 (which equates to $253 per month on a blended
basis) to calculate the US TRx ramp implied by consensus forecasts for the Wegovy Pill in 2026 (Exhibit 61). We have also
included our own TRx ramp, the first year of Zepbound's launch, and the current Wegovy Pill scripts total. The launch so far
has also landed above consensus expectations.

. We sit below consensus for Oral Sema's launch year of TRx volumes. We think that the above expectations launch will
normalize towards the end of the year as a) the initial momentum garnered from the "all-in" marketing approach will fade and
b) the launch of orforglipron coming in 2H26.

. We think the Wegovy Pill can prolong Novo's fight in the obesity market, but it won't change its fortunes. Novo has
overcome investor concerns about the Wegovy Pill's US launch, continuing to outperform market expectations (Exhibit 59).
We also take comfort from the early indications that the market is expanding and not just changing structure (Exhibit 60). But
despite this, we remain cautious of the ability of the Wegovy Pill to inflect Novo's group-wide fortunes. In particular, we note
that this launch has not been "cost-free" because of 1/ capex remaining above "steady-state" until the end of the decade 2/
material increases in SG&A to compete with Lilly 3/ negative mix: 'Wegovy in a Pill' is lower margin than injectable Wegovy
because of its lower selling-price per unit and higher COGS due to more API in the Wegovy Pill:

. Despite the strong execution thus far on US TRx volumes, the success of the Wegovy Pill launch has not been cost
free.

. As a reminder, the stakes on the Wegovy Pill launch were so high given the unforeseen challenges of the Wegovy
Injectable launch. In 2021, Novo were first to market with a weekly GLP-1 for obesity in the US, but insufficient supply of
semaglutide allowed competitors such as Lilly, through Zepbound, and compounding pharmacies, through compounded
semaglutide, to take share.

. Capex is set to remain elevated for the next few years. Considering the supply constraints Novo faced with the
Wegovy injectable, a $25bn capex program was initiated in 2022 to expand Novo's in-house production and fill-finish
capabilities. It is our understanding that we are at the end of this particular capex cycle (which would suggest a peak
capex of 21% of sales in 2025). But we don't expect capex to return to steady state levels (of ~5% of sales) until 2029 as
sites continue to ramp up.

. Management said they went "all-in" on the launch. So, by management's own admission, this is best the Wegovy Pill
could've performed. The launch has taken significant resources and investment, including extensive advertising on the
New York Subway and at the Super Bowl. We think the advertising push has contributed to the Pill's successful launch.

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->

<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

But we think this level of momentum and investment is unsustainable over the medium and long term - especially when
orforglipron enters the market (expected launch could be as early as 2Q26).

. We also note that the orals market will face the same pricing pressures as the injectables, but from a lower base
and lower margin. We assume the same - 10% price deflation in the oral segment as we do in the injectables from 2027
onwards. The first two doses of the Wegovy Pill cost $149 in the cash channel (the first two doses of the injectable are
$199), but the amount of active pharmaceutical ingredient (API) is much higher in the Wegovy Pill, meaning that the gross
margin is considerably lower on a per-unit basis.

## . What else do you need to know about the Wegovy Pill?

· The Wegovy (injectable; 2.4mg) and the Wegovy Pill, have comparable weight loss (-15% vs - 14%; STEP-1 and OASIS-4
respectively). We believe that some patients will prefer to take a daily oral because it is more discreet than taking an
injection, although others will prefer the convenience of a "one-and-done" approach they can have with the weekly
injection. As the weight loss is comparable, we think these effects will net out.

. Semaglutide is a peptide, which means that it has relatively low bioavailability. As a result, the Wegovy Pill requires much
more API (active pharmaceutical ingredient) than the injectable version. Reformulating into a tablet has required Novo to
develop a proprietary technology to ensure that the stomach doesn't completely break down the semaglutide before it
can be absorbed into the bloodstream. (We understand from Novo that the technical challenges here were significant).

. The Wegovy Pill comes will a 30-minute fasting restriction between taking the pill and eating. In our view, this will not be a
significant impediment for patients, given the same restrictions apply to Rybelsus (which has become $3bn drug despite
being launched during the pandemic and receiving limited marketing resources). The Wegovy Pill package insert benefits
from the exact same positive (relative risk reduction of 20%) cardiovascular outcomes' data (SELECT) as the Wegovy
injectable.

· Orforglipron is due to come onto the US market by 2Q26. Orforglipron was invented by Chugai (O; Miki Sogi) and
licensed to Lilly in 2018. As orforglipron is a non-peptide, it doesn't have the same bioavailability challenge as the Wegovy
Pill. We also expect the orforglipron CVOT phase 3 data to be published in March 2026.

· With orforglipron, across the 6mg, 12mg, 36mg doses, the ATTAIN-1 P3 trial recorded -7.5%, -8.4%, and - 11.2% weight
loss. The headline weight loss compares poorly to Zepbound (-20%) and both formulations of Wegovy (-15% and - 14%
for the injectable and oral respectively). In ACHIEVE-3, orforglipron demonstrated much stronger weight loss than Oral
Sema (-9.2% vs -5.3%). Whilst we do not have the full publication (so haven't been able to fully scrutinize the data), we
expect orforglipron to be the dominant oral in the market in terms of script volumes (Exhibit 63) and in US sales (Exhibit
64). We expect the former to command 80% of the US volume share between the two drugs by2036e.

. If the weight-loss data is "muddy" (i.e. no clear winner), why do we project orforglipron to enjoy a much higher share than
Wegovy Pill? Well, we believe that Lilly's sales force benefits from stronger economic incentives because of historic stock
price performance. Our channel checks indicate that its much more difficult to retain good sales people during periods of
share price underperformance because of the resulting reduction in the value of stock options. We understand that for US
bio-pharma employees, stock options are very important component of total financial remuneration.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

# 3/ Compounders finally out?

. A quick recap: why are there compounders in the US market? The FDA allowed compounders into the US obesity
market under section 503B, which allows compounding in cases where there is a supply shortage of the molecule. In
February 2025, the FDA officially announced there was no longer a semaglutide supply issue. Since then, compounders
have been slow to retreat from the market (our channel checks indicated that these discounters still account for 33% of
prescription volumes in FY25).

. We believe that 2026 is the year compounders will be pushed out by the FDA. The strategy employed by Novo (file
lawsuits and cut prices) failed to make a material change to the staying power of compounding pharmacies in FY25 (despite
filing 140 lawsuits and already having won >40 of them). But in our view, the FDA's announcement in February 2026, marks
a significant shift in the willingness of the FDA to take action. We believe that a) compounders will be unable to enter the orals
market at all and that b) their share of total prescription volumes will fall sharply this year (Exhibit 66).

## KEY THEMES ACROSS TO 2036

### 1/ Orals Endgame: What Share Will Orals command?

. The launch of obesity orals (tablets) has attracted a lot of attention, but don't forget the importance of injectables.
Overall, we project the orals market will peak at 35% of the total US obesity TRx volumes, and begin to trend down thereafter
as assets currently in the pipeline come to market (Exhibit 68). We think at "steady-state" (but beyond our forecast horizon)
orals should account for 20% of the total TRx volumes globally. In 2036E, we forecast that orals will account for 30% of US
TRx volumes (Exhibit 69).

. We don't forecast orals to replace injectables as the primary weight-loss medication in the US. Our reasoning is
twofold. First, the vast majority of pipeline assets are weekly injectables. In our obesity model (Exhibit 84), we include 19
"branded" obesity products on the market and in the pipeline, as well as compounding drugs and biosimilars for products
containing semaglutide. Of these, 5 have oral formulations modeled. Second, we are yet to see an oral consistently achieve
better weight loss than an injectable and, while the patients continue to value weight-loss above all else, we think orals
comprising 30% of total TRx volumes in the US seems fair (Exhibit 69). Click here to go to our section on Wegovy Pill (oral
semaglutide) vs orforglipron.

### 2/ Semaglutide Loss of Exclusivity in the US (2032)

. Semaglutide (active ingredient in Wegovy, Wegovy Pill, Ozempic, Rybelsus and CagriSema) loses exclusivity in
the US in 2032. Novo has already lost (or is about to lose) compound exclusivity over semaglutide in several key locations in
2026 which represent 1/3 of the world's obese-adult population (Exhibit 70).

. As of February 2026, products containing the semaglutide molecule has generated revenues of > DKK 100bn
for Novo since Ozempic's launch in 2017. This molecule is the active pharmaceutical ingredient (API) in four products
currently on the market (Exhibit 71) and with the launch of Wegovy Pill and CagriSema (Cagrillinitde + Semaglutide;
launches expected in 2027 (obesity) and 2028 (diabetes) we forecast further portfolio concentration in the short run
(Exhibit 72). It is worth noting that our increase in non-sema revenues are driven primarily by the fall in sema revenues.

. We have modeled a very benign loss of exclusivity impact across products containing semaglutide. We have
included no additional price deflation (maintaining - 10% across 2027-36) and modest volumes declines across Wegovy
injectable, Wegovy Pill, and CagriSema (Exhibit 73). We thus believe that our estimates might, if anything, be on the
conservative side. Our forecasting here has been guided by three factors:

. Ultra efficient production processes: Novo uses a yeast-based manufacturing method to produce semaglutide at large
scale. (Novo has applied its learning's from decades of insulin manufacturing to perfect the sema production process).

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="35" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->
<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->

Based on our recent call with an independent bio-manufacturing expert, we understand that this gives Novo a structural
cost advantage over potential generics entrants. In addition, we have already seen that Novo is willing, when needed, to
compete aggressively on price in the US. But we have not modeled any additional price competition post-LOE.

. High API in the Wegovy Pill: Our channel checks have also indicated that the low bio-availability of semaglutide may
soften the impact of generic competition in the oral market. In particular, we believe that the low bio-availability and lower
average price (to the injectable) means the margin on the oral is much lower than on the injectable (even if storage and
lack of device may partially offset the COGS hit). We have not modeled negative prescription growth for Wegovy Pill post
LOE, but have modeled a slowing in prescription growth rates.

. CagriSema in the Mix: We go into much more detail on CagriSema (obesity; here, diabetes; here), but we understand
that combining Cagrilintide and Semaglutide is difficult due to the differences in pH (semaglutide is stable around pH
7 whereas Cagrilintide is stable around a pH 4). Our channel checks have indicated this could be a rate limiting step for
generic entry.

### 3/ New product launches

. Excluding the role compounders play, we expect the number of obesity drugs on the market to double between
FY25 and FY26. By 2032, we think there could be >20 drugs on the market (Exhibit 74). This includes biosimilars for
semaglutide containing products from 2032. We believe the launches of new products will help drive market growth. We
thus forecast that 70% of the US obesity market value in 2036 is currently not on the market (Exhibit 75).

. We expect CagriSema (Novo) and retatrutide (Lilly) to launch in 2027. In 2028, we expect the first non-Novo, non-Lilly
obesity drugs to enter the market (survodutide; Boehringer Ingelheim/ Zealand Pharma (NC)) and MariTide; Amgen (MP;
covered by Courtney Breen)).

#### CagriSema: consensus is too optimistic in our view:

CagriSema combines semaglutide with an amylin analogue called cagrilintide. Novo expects to launch CagriSema in obesity in
2027, and in diabetes in 2028. The product is formulated as a dual chamber pen because of the difference in acidity between
the two molecules (as referenced earlier). In our view the key controversies surrounding CagriSema in obesity prior are:

· Target market (more of a niche than a super-blockbuster): based on our channel checks, we understand that CagriSema
is not going to be pitched as a mass-market obesity medication (i.e. it is not the "next Wegovy"). Instead, CagriSema is going
to be targeted at patients who need really need extra weight loss. This thus believe that CagriSema is likely to become more
of a "niche" obesity product than what is discounted by consensus. We have modeled a higher price (2x the average of
Wegovy and Zepbound blended net prices in year of launch; $730) but much lower year 1 TRx numbers to reflect the smaller
patient population Exhibit 77.

We think CagriSema will account for 20% of Novo's obesity portfolio (CagriSema, Wegovy, and Wegovy Pill) by 2036e (Exhibit
78) and sales will peak at 50% of Wegovy Pill sales. We think this reflects CagriSema's positioning as a "niche" obesity product.

. In contrast, we think consensus views CagriSema more like a "Wegovy replacement" than a niche product. Using consensus
numbers from Bloomberg, we estimate that consensus thinks CagriSema will account for 38% of Novo's obesity portfolio
(CagriSema, Wegovy, and Wegovy Pill) will peak sales matching those of Wegovy Pill. In our view, this would make CagriSema
a mass-market obesity medication which, from Novo's IR team, we understand will not be the case.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

. REDEFINE-4: this head-to-head phase 3 trial of CagriSema vs Zepbound (reported in February 2026). The importance of
the trial was elevated after CagriSema missed investors' weight loss expectations in REDEFINE-1 in December 2024 (Novo
was expecting to CagriSema to be superior to Zepbound and achieve 25% weight loss. But CagriSema only achieved 23%
weight loss).

· In REDEFINE-4, CagriSema again failed to meet 25% weight loss (achieving 23%, Zepbound achieved 25% weight loss)
and also failed to meet the primary end-point of non-inferiority (i.e. the trial showed CagriSema is inferior to Zepbound).
We don't see how CagriSema can be a big disruptor in the obesity market going forwards. We are still waiting for full
publication of the trial results at the time of writing.

· In Exhibit 80, we compare weight loss and discontinuation due to adverse events across REDEFINE-1 (CagriSema) and
SURMOUNT-1 trial (Zepbound). While the focus has been on REDEFINE-4, management have advised that we may not
see peak weight loss until REDEFINE-11 (which should report in 3Q26). But we do not think any further phase III trials
data is likely to materially change the positioning of CagriSema as a niche obesity product.

. Generic competition: Despite being used in combination with cagrilintide, CagriSema will be at risk of generic competition
in the US when semaglutide molecule patent expires in 2032. We understand that the dual chamber (DC) mechanism will
not be a specific barrier to entry post semaglutide LOE, although our channel checks have indicated that the DC will make
production ramp up slower than is normally the case for injectables. We also note that the cagrilintide patent will last well into
the late 2030s (Novo have not confirmed when).

<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

# US DIABETES MARKET:

## SIMILAR DYNAMICS, SIMILAR CONCLUSIONS

Ozempic is Novo's largest product by global revenue (Exhibit 85) but we forecast yoy revenue declines even prior
to the US semaglutide LOE (2032). In our forecasts, the cumulative revenues from (global) sales of Wegovy, Oral Sema,
CagriSema (in obesity and diabetes) and all other portfolio and pipeline assets will only just compensate falling Ozempic
revenues (Exhibit 86).

## The US Most Favored Nation (MFN) pricing and TrumpRx deals included Ozempic and Mounjaro (Lilly's GLP-1 for

diabetes). Thus, we expect contractions in the net price of a similar magnitude to the obesity market (which you re-review
here). Ozempic is now available at around $350 per month on TrumpRx and will be available at $250/month via US Medicare
(everyone with Type 2 Diabetes (TD2) >65 years of age eligible) and US Medicaid (all households with income below the Federal
Poverty Level eligible) from April 2026.

We do not believe that the cash channel will be as important in diabetes as it is in obesity. However, we expect lower cash
channel prices (and the lower medicare and medicaid prices) to have spillover effects into the commercial channel. We expect
this to result in a lowering of net US diabetes drug prices. In our detailed bottom up US diabetes model, we forecast -30% US
price declines in FY26,-15% in 2027, and -10% across 2028-36. Similar to the obesity market, 10-20% yoy price declines are
normal in the diabetes segment in the US.

Unlike in Obesity, we don't model an inflection of GLP-1 prescriptions in 2027, despite the steep price cuts in 2026.
In our view, the GLP-1 segment of the US diabetes market is much more mature than obesity (we are approaching a decade
of Ozempic use in the US; launched in 2017) and is far less geared towards to the "consumer". Put simply, the pool of patients
actively seeking weight loss is substantially larger than the pool focused solely on tighter glycemic control. Further, we note that
many diabetes patients may wish to use Wegovy (or Zepbound) instead of Ozempic (or Mounjaro) in order to benefit from the
weight loss as well as reductions in HbA 1c levels (the active molecule in Zepbound and Mounjaro is the same; tirzepatide).

EXHIBIT 85: Ozempic accounted for 41% of group sales in EXHIBIT 86: Novo sources of revenue growth 2025-32

The diabetes market also has multiple established drug classes (e.g. metformin, SGLT2 inhibitors, DPP-4 inhibitors,
GLP-1/GIP agonists, insulins), and combination regimens are common as disease progresses. Metformin (been off patent for
\>20 years in the US), a biguanide, remains first-line for most patients, with early addition of SGLT2 inhibitors (soon to be off-

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="41" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

patent in the US too) and/or GLP-1 receptor agonists in those with cardiovascular disease, heart failure, chronic kidney disease,
or obesity. Other medications, such as DPP-4 inhibitors, sulfonylureas and pioglitazone (which are also all off-patent in the US)
are used as alternatives or add-on therapies depending on comorbidities, glycemic control, tolerability and cost.

We have witnessed GLP-1s take an increasing share of US prescription volumes in the diabetes market, and we think
this will continue, albeit at a slower rate across 2026-36 (Exhibit 87). We expect SGLT-2s and GLP-1s to gain 22% market
share (+10% and +12% respectively). Many SGLT-2s will lose patent protection over the next few years (e.g. Farxiga (LOE
in 2027); AstraZeneca (O)) and are already priced relatively cheaply. Thus, we forecast an increasing share of diabetes TRx
volumes for this class over the next decade in the US. We also forecast that GLP-1 medications will account for 44% of TRx
volumes by 2036 (up from 32% in 2025).

Within GLP-1s, we think Mounjaro will continue to take share from Ozempic (Exhibit 88). We see a similarly entrenched
trend in the US diabetes market as we have described above in the US obesity market. In our view, Mounjaro achieves superior

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="42" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->
<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->

Hb 1Ac reduction (Exhibit 90) and this has been reflected in the volume share battle seen since 2020 (Exhibit 89). We think it is
unlikely that these trends will change significantly over the medium-and-long term.

# CAGRISEMA: IT WON'T BE ENOUGH

In the wider GLP-1 market, we think CagriSema can help stabilize Novo's share loss in the diabetes market. We
understand that Novo will launch CagriSema in diabetes post the launch in obesity (we expect the diabetes launch to be in
2028). In our model, using the same price assumed in our obesity model ($730 - a +100% premium to our assumed blended
net price of Ozempic and Mounjaro) and a similar TRx curve to CagriSema in obesity, we forecast CagriSema to have a peak
share of 3% of the total US diabetes TRx and 7% of GLP-1 TRx (Exhibit 89).

REDEFINE-3 (read out in February 2026) demonstrated that patients taking CagriSema achieved greater reductions
in Hb 1Ac than patients using semaglutide. We're not aware of any head-to-head data published vs tirzepatide yet (and our
understanding is that REDFINE-4 did not have a comparative Hb 1Ac reduction as a secondary endpoint), but an indirect ('meta')
analysis between REDEFINE-3 and SURPASS-1 suggests to us that Mounjaro (tirzepatide) has more effective Hb 1Ac reduction
(Exhibit 90).

All things considered, we think that CagriSema is better diabetes drug than Ozempic, but Mounjaro will remain
the leader. We believe that this is reflected fairly in our 2036 market share forecasts (Exhibit 89). On our estimates, diabetes
revenues will comprise 60-70% of "total" CagriSema revenues (taking the sum of both obesity and diabetes) - Exhibit 91.

# APPENDIX 1: RARE DISEASE

## MASH/EFRUXIFERMIN

In 3Q25, Novo Nordisk confirmed its acquisition of Akero therapeutics for ~$5.2Bn in cash and contingent value rights to
gain access to its lead asset efruxifermin. The drug is a bivalent fibroblast growth factor 21 (FGF21) and beta-klotho receptor
analogue used in the treatment of Metabolic-dysfunction Associated Steato-Hepatitis (MASH). MASH is a disease of the liver
with a high unmet clinical need, and we believe efruxifermin will be a best-in class drug.

## What is MASH?

Metabolic-dysfunction Associated Steato-Hepatitis (MASH), previously known as Non-Alcoholic Steato-Hepatitis (NASH), is a
disease process involving the build up of excess fatty tissues within the liver. The fat is deposited through a variety of biological
processes, and can irritate healthy liver cells and cause inflammation and scarring to build up.

Scarring in the liver is more broadly known as fibrosis, or cirrhosis in severe cases which can significantly increase the risk of
liver cancer. MASH can cause fibrosis, and although the two are strongly linked are strictly speaking separate. Hence, one can
exist without the other.

Source: AstraZeneca, Bernstein Analysis

The exact cause of MASH is unknown, and a multitude of metabolic factors are thought to contribute such as obesity, diabetes
and high cholesterol levels. Although these other health problems do not necessarily lead to MASH, they are ubiquitous in MASH
patients.

Source: 89bio

In the US alone, approximately 25% of the population will develop metabolic-dysfunction associated steatotic liver disease
(MASLD), a milder version of MASH in their lifetime, with 20-25% of those patients progressing to MASH (Exhibit 95). The
prevalence of MASH in the US is predicted to increase 63% from 16.5 million cases in 2015 to 27.0 million cases in 2030, with
similar rates across Europe.

MASH can often be silent with no symptoms, hence the discrepancy between the prevalence of the disease and the number of
patients actually diagnosed and treated.

Those who do have symptoms suffer with tiredness, general weakness, some discomfort in the tummy and occasionally
some itching and bruising. Although by itself it is rarely a debilitating disease, MASH is a stepping stone to more severe
scarring leading to cancer and death. As such, it is considered a serious health problem, and the second leading driver of liver
transplantation worldwide.

### Disease burden and treatment paradigm

Given MASH often forms part of a constellation of health problems, estimating its economic impact can be difficult.
Nevertheless, conservative estimates place the medical costs of MASH at $78.6Bn by 2040 in the US alone, whilst MASH
associated productivity loss will be close to $246Bn during same timescale. Per patient medical costs in the US are expected
to be $3,601 by 2040, whilst individual patient productivity loss annually is expected to top $11,300 in the same year.
Epidemiological research by Madrigal Therapeutics (NR; a leader in MASH), demonstrates that in addition to a growing
MASH patient population, the percentage of patients being treated will also grow substantially in the medium to long term as
awareness of the disease increases.

To diagnose MASH, physicians may use bloodwork to examine liver function tests, ultrasound scans, and (rarely) a biopsy
(removal of a sample via surgery) of the liver are conducted to confirm the disease pathology. With progress in ultrasound
technology and protocols, the need for biopsy has reduced significantly and clinicians are growing increasingly confident in
treating the disease in the absence of a confirmed tissue sample.

In terms of treatment, a healthy diet and physical activity is often the first line to minimize circulating fats in the bloodstream, and
mitigate the metabolic risk factors contributing to MASH.

Bariatric surgery (forced stomach shrinkage by decreasing the diameter of the gastric tract) to treat obesity is evidenced to be
up to 90% effective in treating MASH. Meanwhile, liver transplantation can be offered in cases of MASH where liver function is
most severely restricted.

Medical (i.e. drug) treatment usually starts with cholesterol lowering medication and good diabetic control. Although medical
treatment specific to MASH is uncommon with only a few approved agents licensed for the disease.

### Current therapeutic landscape

Rezdiffra (Madrigal therapeutics; unrated) is a drug that mimics the thyroid hormone to promote fat breakdown and dissolution.
More specifically, Rezdiffra binds to the thyroid hormone receptor beta (THR-beta) in liver fat to promote its breakdown.

In the phase III MAESTRO-NASH trial program, Rezdiffra was compared to placebo to assess fibrosis and MASH on ultrasound
imaging. 25.9% of patients achieved at least a 1 stage improvement in their F score without worsening of MASH compared to
14.2% in the placebo group (p<0.001). No concerning safety signals were reported and the drug was approved as the first
ever licensed treatment exclusively for MASH in 1Q2024. Following sales of $180m in 2024, consensus (Bloomberg) projects
Rezdiffra peak sales of $5Bn in 2031 for a CAGR (25-31e) of 61%.

The second, and only other medication with an indication in MASH is semaglutide, known as Wegovy (Novo Nordisk; O), an
analogue of the glucagon like peptide 1 (GLP-1) receptor. This drug acts on the hormone pathway that promotes appetite
suppression, lowers blood sugar levels and improves control of body weight. Originally indicated for tackling obesity, the
phase III ESSENCE study trialed Wegovy against placebo in MASH patients, evaluating whether there was improvement in liver
stiffness and fibrosis reflected on ultrasound imaging and bloodwork.

A reduction in liver fibrosis without worsening of MASH was reported in 36.8% of patients taking Wegovy compared to 22.4%
in the placebo group (p<0.001; Exhibit 97). This was a clinically meaningful result that led to Wegovy's FDA approval for MASH
in August 2025. Although Wegovy commanded sales of DKK 58.2Bn in FY24, sales specific to the MASH indication are forecast
by Bernstein to peak at DKK 5Bn in 2032e after which biosimilar ('generic') competition is expected.

In our view the adverse event profile of Wegovy in ESSENCE mirrors that of this drug's use in obesity, i.e highly favorable with no
concerning safety signal (Exhibit 98).

In our view MASH has a high economic and quality of life cost to patients and society. This is because we believe that this
disease is not adequately managed by current treatments. MASH is the second leading cause for liver transplants worldwide,
setting up a potential future bottleneck (as there simply aren't enough liver donors). As such, we believe that the MASH
pharmacotherapeutic space has significant unmet clinical need, high commercial potential and there is a clamor for new
entrants to the market.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

<!-- PageHeader="Justin Smith +44 20 7762 5899 justin.smith@bernsteinsg.com" -->
<!-- PageHeader="18 March 2026" -->

### Spotlight on efruxifermin

Efruxifermin is a member of a new class of MASH medications that mimic the activity of fibroblast growth factor 21 (FGF21 .
FGF21 is a naturally occurring hormone that is often described as a "master metabolic regulator" (Exhibit 99) which controls
and reduces fat accumulation throughout the body and thus comprises healthy metabolism. This drug is administered by
subcutaneous (under the skin) injection on a weekly basis.

By providing an analogue that is longer acting than the
hormone present in the body, efruxifermin prolongs and
amplifies the positive effects of normally present FGF21. Some
of these desirable effects include healing of the scar tissue in
the liver, and slowing down the MASH disease process.

With MASH induced fibrosis in particular, FGF21 has
demonstrated impressive potential in vitro (in a test tube).
As such, we believe this platform is of particular interest in
next generation MASH therapy, and we believe that it could
compete within the sparsely populated treatment landscape
for MASH.

In the phase IIb HARMONY study, efruxifermin reduced fibrosis
by >1 F stage in at least 75% of patients compared to 24%
in placebo (p<0.001). No concerning safety signals were
reported and the adverse event profile was favorable in our
view.

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

This primary endpoint in HARMONY was measured on liver
histology at 96 weeks and given the statistically significant
positive results, efruxifermin was in our view a clinically
meaningful treatment that will affect the standard of care.
Efruxifermin is currently in three different phase III trials
(SYNCHRONY program), evaluating the same endpoints as
in phase II but in more severe MASH patients and those with
cirrhosis already established. The first phase 3 trial in this
program is expected to read out in 1H26.
