In addition to the FGF21 receptor, efruxifermin is unique in this competitive landscape as it also partially stimulated the beta-
klotho receptor. This receptor has an even broader role across metabolic cellular processes than FGF21, and in combination
with FGF21, we believe efruxifermin is a unique and effective therapeutic approach. No concerning safety signals have been
reported thus far.

## Competitive landscape

In this exciting time for entrants into the MASH therapy landscape, several other assets are also in phase III which, in our view,
have demonstrated results in phase II that are similar to efruxifermin.

In September 2025 Roche announced the acquisition of 89bio (unrated) for $3.5Bn including contingent value rights for
89bio's lead asset pegozafermin, an FGF21 analogue designed to treat fatty diseases of the liver, in particular MASH. This
drug's phase III trials are set to read out in 1H28, and Roche is guiding to >3Bn CHF in nominal peak year sales, its highest
guidance bucket.

In phase II thus far, the ENLIVEN trials tested three different dosages of pegozafermin (15,30 and 44mg) against placebo,
reporting results in 2023. The trial evaluated as its primary endpoint an improvement in F stage on liver ultrasound as well as
biopsy both at baseline and at the conclusion of the trial (Exhibit 102).

At 24 weeks, a statistically significant increase in the odds of improving fibrosis by at least one F stage at the intermediate
pegozafermin dose of 30mg weekly (Relative risk 3.7, 1.2-10.8 p<0.009) was reported.

At the lowest dose of 15mg weekly, the chance of resolving MASH completely and without worsening of fibrosis significantly
increased (RR 18.9, 3.2-113.1 p <0.001).

In addition to the imaging measures of success already reported, the blood levels of alanine amino transferase (ALT) - a
surrogate for liver damage - also saw sustained declines through to the end of the study. In the most severe F4 patients with
borderline cirrhosis, nearly all experienced a significant improvement in their F scores which brought their livers back from the
brink of cancer to a more healthy state.

With respect to adverse events, the profile of pegozafermin was generally favorable in ENLIVEN and no concerning safety
signals were reported. The majority of events centered around diarrhea and nausea, and there were almost no events severe
enough to warrant stoppage of treatment (p value not reported).

In phase III, 89bio is running the ENLIGHTEN fibrosis and ENLIGHTEN cirrhosis trials which are both set to read out in 2H28.
The fibrosis trial is similar to the phase II trial, with an improvement of F stage as its primary endpoint. In addition, ENLIGHTEN
fibrosis will add a greater number of biopsies to its protocol over multiple years, to support its ultrasound based conclusions.

The pegozafermin cirrhosis phase III study has similar intervention arms and timescales, except that the primary endpoint is now
an improvement in cirrhosis on biopsy at 24months and 60 months. We believe that Pegozafermin will be the main competitor to
efruxifermin in the long run, with efruxifermin benefitting from an earlier read out date and potential first-in-class advantage.

GSK (O) recently announced its purchase of efimosfermin alfa, another long acting FGF21 analogue medication invented by
Boston Pharmaceuticals. In phase II, the drug demonstrated at least a 30% reduction in an ultrasound measure of liver fat in
89% of patients, compared to just 7% in the placebo group. This effect was dose dependent, with up to 100% reduction in the
highest dose group.

The tolerability profile was favorable, with 66% of patients experiencing mild treatment related adverse events compared to
49% in the placebo group. In Phase III, the ZENITH 1 and 2 trials are testing two dosage levels of the drug on patients with
biopsy confirmed MASH to evaluate improvement in F stage and number of adverse events as their primary endpoints. These
trials will each last 52 weeks, are currently recruiting and estimated readouts will be in 2H28.

Survodutide (Boehringer Ingelheim (private company)) is a dual GLP1 and glucagon analogue. (Glucagon is the hormone which
raises blood sugar when levels fall too low, promoting the breakdown of stored sugar (glycogen) in the liver. Designed with a
focus on MASH, phase II evidence for this drug demonstrated resolution of MASH with no worsening of fibrosis stage in 62%
patients compared to 14% in the placebo group (p<0.001). No concerning safety signals were reported with adverse events at
only 8% in the intervention arms compared to 7% in placebo.

Two phase III trials are ongoing through the LIVERAGE program, with primary endpoints of all cause liver dysfunction and
mortality at 76 weeks post treatment. These trials are set to read out in 1H29.

Tirzepatide (Eli Lilly; Breen; O), is a dual agonist of the GLP-1 and gastric inhibitory polypeptide (GIP) receptors and has been
explored for MASH. In phase II, the SYNERGY-NASH trial found that up to 62.4% (p<0.001) of patients experienced resolution
of MASH without worsening of F stage at 52 weeks post treatment on biopsy. Having met its primary end-point, with no safety
signals reported, the drug awaits registration into phase III trials. (This drug is already FDA approved for use in diabetes (brand

name = Mounjaro) and obesity ((brand name = Zepbound)).

MASH has for a long time been a graveyard for pharmaceutical R&D. The previous need for biopsy left a diagnostic bottleneck
that has now been resolved with advances in ultrasound in our view. Given the overall increase in metabolic disorders in recent
decades, these factors have created the environment for a sustained increase in the number of MASH patients which, in our
view, shows no sign of slowing.

In our view, evaluating the extent of the economic and clinical unmet need in MASH specifically, as opposed to its comorbid
conditions is difficult. Nonetheless, we believe that this unmet need is material. In our view, FGF21 is an evident next step in the
treatment paradigm for MASH, and we believe that efruxifermin may be a potential best in class medication.

## APPENDIX 2: A GENERALIST'S GUIDE TO GLP-15: WITH A ZOOM IN ON SEMAGLUTIDE

### The Main Mechanisms of Action

Obesity drugs, in general, target our "hunger-related" hormones. As the body's digestive system (and it's links with the
brain) are extremely complicated, there are numerous hormones needed for the body to function. A drug (such as semaglutide;
active ingredient in Wegovy) may only target a single hormone (and is termed a "single agonist") others (such as tirzepatide,
active ingredient in Zepbound) target two hormones ("double agonist"). Below we list the main mechanisms of actions:

· GLP-1 receptor agonist. Mimics the Glucagon-Like Peptide 1 (a hormone) by inhibiting the release of glucagon (hormone
raising blood sugar levels) and stimulating the production of insulin (a hormone that lowers blood sugar levels) thus reducing
hyperglycemia which leads to lower blood sugar levels. Furthermore, GLP-1s reduce hunger and slow the GI (gastro
intestinal) food flow leading to a decrease in appetite.

. Amylin receptor agonist. Mimics amylin (hormone secreted with insulin) which inhibits glucagon secretion and affects
gastric emptying plus food intake.

· GIP receptor agonist. Mimics the Glucose-dependant Insulinotropic Peptide (hormone) which stimulates insulin production
and sensitivity leading to a better insulin penetration in the bloodstream, thus lowering blood sugar levels. The hormone also
reduces stomach acid production, leading to a slow GI food flow resulting in a decrease in appetite.

. Glucagon receptor agonist. Mimics glucagon which increases blood glucose levels and breaks down lipids and amino
acids. Exogenous intake of glucagon leads to increased energy expenditure with a lower food intake.

· CB1 receptor inverse agonist. The peripheral cannabinoid receptor 1 modulates appetite and energy homeostasis but also
metabolic inflammation induced by insulin resistance.

### Side Effects and Tolerance

The most common side effects from GLP-1s are gastro-intestinal (GI) problems. The primary mechanism of action of
GLP-1s is to alter the way our "hunger hormones" behave and interact with each other. It is, therefore, unsurprising that gut-
related issues are the primary side effect from taking GLP-1s.

. Nausea, vomiting, constipation, bloating, and digestive reflux are all common GI adverse events. Especially towards
the start of treatment, where the body's digestive process is undergoing a "re-wiring", side effects may manifest themselves.
For most people, the side effects are manageable (or tolerated for the benefit of the weight loss).

. Fatigue, headaches, dizziness, and taste changes can also be caused by GLP-1 usage. As the body readjusts to a new
level of caloric intake, the body undergoes a "metabolic stress test". These side effects can be prominent when people are
adjusting to new dosing levels, and can exacerbated by dehydration (GLP-1s also suppress the "thirst hormones").

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

Whilst we think the tolerance profile of a drug is important, the main driver behind the success of an anti-obesity
product remains headline the weight loss. Patients are willing to accept a worse tolerance profile in order to achieve larger
amounts of weight loss. Most drugs (including those in the pipeline) have a similar discontinuation due to adverse events rates
\- certainly not different enough to shape patient behavior. We think the weight loss-tolerance trade off will become more
important as more drugs enter the market, but weight-loss will remain the driving force.

### Semaglutide: A Deep(er) Dive

Semaglutide is a long-acting glucagon-like peptide 1 receptor agonist (GLP-1 RA). Glucagon-like peptide-1 is a naturally
occurring hormone that helps the body regulate blood sugar (glucose) levels, appetite, and the speed at which nutrients are
moved from the stomach into the bloodstream.

Semaglutide is a modified version of the naturally occurring hormone. The GLP-1 drugs increase the length of time it lasts in the
body and activates the GLP-1 receptors (starting signals) more steadily. Semaglutide has three primary effects on the body:

1\. Pancreas: Increases the amount of insulin secreted when glucose is high (normally just after eating) but not when glucose
levels are low (lowering the risk of hypoglycemic - crashes in blood sugar levels - attacks).

2\. Stomach: Slows gastric emptying (slows the rate that nutrients enter the bloodstream) which "smooths" out the body's
glucose response. This lowers strain on the pancreas (reducing the risk of developing diabetes in the first instance, as well
as slowing the pancreatic strain in diabetes patients) and helps patients feel "fuller" for longer.

<!-- PageFooter="EUROPEAN BIOPHARMACEUTICALS" -->
<!-- PageFooter="BERNSTEIN SOCIETE GENERALE GROUP" -->
<!-- PageNumber="55" -->
<!-- PageBreak -->

<figure>

3\. Brain: Increases the body's "fullness" signal and reduces the "hungry" signals. This means that patients feel they need to eat
less (both in volume and frequency).

# VALUATION METHODOLOGY

## Novo Nordisk A/S

Our price target (DKK175) is the average of an EV/EBITA-multiples based valuation (applying a 36% discount to 2026-28e peer
group) and DCF (WACC 8.0%, terminal growth 0%).

## RISKS

### Novo Nordisk A/S

Upside risks: 1. The major FDA approved growth drivers (Ozempic, Wegovy and Oral-Sema) and pipeline (in particular Cagri-Sema)
deliver the consensus estimates 2. Novo's manufacturing scale means that patent expiries are even more benign than we expect
3. Management engages in major, value accretive, bridging M&A.

# RATINGS DEFINITIONS, BENCHMARKS AND DISTRIBUTION

## EQUITY RATINGS DEFINITIONS

### Bernstein brand

The Bernstein brand rates stocks based on forecasts of relative performance for the next 12 months versus the S&P 500 for
stocks listed on the U.S. and Canadian exchanges, versus the Bloomberg Europe Developed Markets Large and Mid Cap Price
Return Index EUR (EDME) for stocks listed on the European exchanges and emerging markets exchanges outside of the Asia
Pacific region, versus the Bloomberg Japan Large and Mid Cap Price Return Index USD (JPL) for stocks listed on the Japanese
exchanges, and versus the Bloomberg Asia ex-Japan Large and Mid Cap Price Return Index (ASIAX) for stocks listed on the Asian
(ex-Japan) exchanges -unless otherwise specified.

The Bernstein brand has three categories of ratings:

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

· Outperform: Stock will outpace the market index by more than 15 pp

· Market-Perform: Stock will perform in line with the market index to within +/-15 pp

. Underperform: Stock will trail the performance of the market index by more than 15 pp

Coverage Suspended: Coverage of a company under the Bernstein research brand has been suspended. Ratings and price targets
are suspended temporarily, are no longer current, and should therefore not be relied upon.

Not Rated: A rating assigned when the stock cannot be accurately valued, or the performance of the company accurately
predicted, at the present time. The covering analyst may continue to publish research reports on the company to update investors
on events and developments.

Not Covered (NC) denotes companies that are not under coverage.

Bernstein brand stock ratings are based on a 12-month time horizon.

And Financial Services Index (BEBANKS) and Bloomberg Europe Dev Mkt Financials Large and Mid Cap Price Ret Index EUR
(EDMFI) index for developed European banks and Payments, the Bloomberg Europe 500 Insurance Index (BEINSUR) for European
insurers, the S&P 500 and S&P Financials for US banks and Payments coverage, S5LIFE for US Insurance, the S&P Insurance
Select Industry (SPSIINS) for US Non-Life Insurers coverage, and the Bloomberg Emerging Markets Financials Large, Mid and
Small Cap Price Return Index (EMLSF) for emerging market banks and insurers and Payments. Ratings are stated relative to the
sector (not the market).

The Autonomous brand has three categories of common stock ratings:

. Outperform (OP): Stock will outpace the relevant index by more than 10 pp

· Neutral (N): Stock will perform in line with the market index to within +/-10 pp

. Underperform (UP): Stock will trail the performance of the relevant index by more than 10 pp

Coverage Suspended: Coverage of a company under the Autonomous research brand has been suspended. Ratings and price
targets are suspended temporarily, are no longer current, and should therefore not be relied upon.

Not Rated: A rating assigned when the stock cannot be accurately valued, or the performance of the company accurately
predicted, at the present time. The covering analyst may continue to publish research reports on the company to update investors
on events and developments.

Those denoted as 'Feature' (e.g., Feature Outperform FOP, Feature Under Outperform FUP) are our core ideas.

Not Covered (NC) denotes companies that are not under coverage.

Autonomous brand common stock ratings are based on a 12-month time horizon.

### Autonomous brand - preferred stocks

The Autonomous brand has three categories of preferred stock ratings:

<!-- PageFooter="latika.sharma@evalueserve.com - Evalueserve (India) - 14/05/2026 12:58 PM UTC" -->

· Outperform (OP): The total return of the preferred instrument is expected to outperform preferred securities of other issuers
operating in similar sectors or rating categories over the next six months.

· Neutral (N): The total return of the preferred instrument is expected to perform in line with preferred securities of other issuers
operating in similar sectors or rating categories over the next six months.

. Underperform (UP): The total return of the preferred instrument is expected to underperform preferred securities of other
issuers operating in similar sectors or rating categories over the next six months.

Autonomous preferred stock ratings are based on a 6-month time horizon.

home currency, movements in exchange rates will have an effect on the value, either favorable or unfavorable. Before acting on any
